2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國聯準會如市場預期 ，維持政策利率不變。 決策官員仍對通膨保持擔憂 ， 也承
認美國經濟正以「強勁速度」成長。聯準會主席Kevin Warsh在會後談話中，並
未明確排除進一步緊縮政策的可能，激勵市場對12 月升息的預期升溫。數據方
面，美國第三季 GDP 年化成長率達 3.4%，高於前值的 2.2%，亦優於市場預期
的 3.2%，印證美國經濟動能強勁。此外，9 月 PCE 與核心 PCE 符合預期，但凸
顯通膨壓力持續偏高。在 「通膨高企」 與 「經濟強韌」 雙重因素驅動下，市場預
期聯準會將於 12 月升息，為美元指數提供有力支撐。惟近期受 AI 產業股票波
動加劇影響，部分資金轉入美債避險，導致美元指數在高檔面臨調節性賣壓。
⚫ 儘管受能源價格走揚影響，9 月歐元區通膨率升至 3.8%，但在薪資壓力維持溫
和，且歐洲央行尚未觀察到第二輪通膨效應全面擴散的情況下 ，歐洲央行上週決
議維持政策利率不變 。通膨回升令市場預期歐洲央行將在下次會議升息 ， 為歐元

提供部分支撐 ， 但考量歐元區經濟成長表現仍落後美國 ， 在美元基本面強勁的抑
制下 ， 歐元反彈力道相對有限 。 目前歐元最大的潛在風險在於部分國家的債券市
場壓力尚未消散，市場對歐洲財政風險仍保持高度警戒。
⚫ 日本央行維持政策利率於 1.25%不變，並且首次明確表示已達成其 2%的通膨目
標；過去日本央行雖認為通膨改善 ， 但始終強調尚未穩定達標，如今首次正式承
認達成 2%目標，代表未來升息門檻進一步下降。此外，日本央行將經濟成長預
測維持在 0.7%至 0.8%，並小幅上修 CPI 預測，令市場預期日央 12 月有望再次
升息，日本貨幣政策正常化將有助日圓結束疲軟走勢。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.390，低點在 32.676，以 32.478 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 3 分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，市場等待聯準會利率決議，交投偏謹慎觀望，而外
資在前周大幅匯出之後，轉為雙向進出，台幣止跌回穩，最高來到 32.390 的周內高
點；然而周間，美國科技股財報表現失色，台股賣壓沉重，外資賣超台股並再度轉
為大幅匯出，台幣走貶，最低來到 32.676，不過匯價逢高吸引出口商賣壓出籠，加
上央行提供流動性，台幣貶幅收斂，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.478。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣偏弱震盪。就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持利率不
變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，且聯準會表示通膨仍處於高檔，顯示聯準會對通
膨仍保持警戒，市場預期美國利率將維持高檔，台美利差仍維持在相對高位，不利
台幣表現；另一方面，美國科技股財報表現不如預期，引發市場對AI 投資回報的疑
慮，對台股也帶來壓力。就台灣市場來看，外資持續調節台股並大幅匯出，10 月累
計賣超將近兆元，資金匯出壓力大，不過一方面央行持續進場調節，加上匯價逢高
預計吸引出口商進場拋匯，料將限制台幣貶值速度。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢
偏弱。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣持續走升。周初市場等待美國聯準會利率決議，交投偏向觀望，境
外人民幣主要於 6.6600 上方震盪； 周間聯準會如預期維持利率不變 ，美元指數自 102
上方回落 ， 加上中國出口結匯需求持續提供支撐， 境外人民幣突破6.6600 整數關口；
週五人行中間價續創近三年九個月新高，最高來到 6.7138，顯示中國人行允許人民
幣溫和升值的走勢，境外人民幣一度升破 6.6500 整數關口，最高來到 6.6487，終場
收在 6.6528，週線升值 162 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6558，週線升值 146 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升 。 就中國國內市場來看 ，10 月官方製造業 PMI 降至 49.3，
重新落入景氣收縮區間，反映中國內需及製造業動能仍有待改善，不過 9 月出口年
增 36.3%，出口商結匯需求持續提供人民幣支撐，加上人行中間價續創近三年九個
月新高，預計將持續帶動人民幣走升；就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持
利率不變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，顯示聯準會對通膨仍保持警戒，市場預期
美國利率將維持高檔 ， 美中利差維持在相對高位 ，將限制人民幣升值的速度與幅度 ；
最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散 ，匯價維持在五日均
線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣
偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6200-6.6700

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 上漲。週初，美伊衝突仍陷僵局支撐美元避險買盤，USD/JPY 最
高來到 158.34；週間，聯準會公布利率維持利率不變，USD/JPY 持續在 158 附近盤
整；臨近週末，日本央行公布利率維持不變並釋放偏鷹訊號， USD/JPY 最低來到
156.94，終場收在 157.53，週線上漲 72 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本央行於上周五宣布利
率維持在 1.25%不變，並表示隨著基礎通膨率大致達到 2%，央行必須持續控制通
膨穩定於此目標，且再次強調貨幣政策已進入更為緊縮階段，未來每隔數月即可能
升息。此外，日本央行在季度報告中，亦將截至 2028 年 3 月財年的核心消費者通
膨預估自三個月前預測的 2.4%上調至 2.6%。日本央行偏鷹立場令市場預期日本最
快將於 12 月再次升息，預計將有利日圓表現。二、聯準會宣布維持利率於 3.75%-
4%區間不變，惟因通膨仍處高位，聯準會重申將致力實現 2%的長期通膨區間， 
FedWatch 顯示 12 月升息機率約七成，美元持穩，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 下行空
間。三、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 各天期均線趨平，近期匯價大致
在 156 至 158 間震盪，匯價暫無明確方向性。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現
區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日圓：156.00-158.50 
前週交易區間 
156.63 158.34

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美伊衝突持續以及法國政經情勢動盪等情況下，
歐元走跌，最低來到 1.1111；週間，聯準會和歐洲央行相繼公布利率決議均維持不
變，歐洲央行發表言論偏鷹派，歐元止跌回升至 1.1214；臨近週末，歐元區公布經
濟數據好壞參半，歐元自高點回落，終場收在 1.1158，週線下跌 41 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行如市場預期宣布
存款利率維持在 2.5%不變，並警告能源成本持續上漲將對通膨造成上行風險，暗示
可能在 12 月再次升息 ， 升息預期升溫預計將支撐歐元走勢 。二 、 歐元區第三季GDP
年增 1.3%， 高於市場預期的1.1%， 顯示在能源價格持續上漲的不利因素下 ，經濟仍
展現韌性，預計有利歐元表現。三、聯準會宣布維持利率在 3.75%-4%不變，並重申
致力控制通膨的立場 。 此外 ，美國公布第三季 GDP 成長 3.4%， 優於市場預期的3.2%，
9 月核心 PCE 年增率自前月的 3%上升至 3.1%，在美國經濟強勁及通膨仍高的情況
下 ， 市場預期聯準會將於 12 月升息一碼，美元持穩 ，預計將限縮歐元走升空間 。四、
技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 各天期均線向下發散，不過近期匯價在多次向
下測試 1.1120 附近均未有效跌破 ， 顯示下方仍有買盤支撐。綜合以上 ， 預估EUR/USD
本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1050-1.1270 
前週交易區間 
1.1103 1.1226

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.27-115.10.30 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109R 2.03% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.845%~1.846%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 時序邁入月底 ， 銀行間已大致調整好積數 ， 且央行未明顯
增發定存單，令市場資金充裕。近兩日股市回落，令週五資金需求上揚，惟供
需尚屬平衡，因此短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.825% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.66%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 本週台債市場交投依舊清淡 ，10 年期指標券 A15109R 等殖系統成交在 2.03%，
5 年期指標券 A15108 議價成交在 1.845%~1.846%。 
美債利率自波段高點回落，且央行總裁楊金龍本週立法院發言維持近來對通
膨容忍度變寬之態度 ， 惟市場對央行升息預期並未完全消除 ， 買盤追價動力有
限，預期台債殖利率維持區間整理，關注下週30 年期公債標售果，預估 5 年
券區間為 1.80%~1.85%，10 年券區間為 1.99%~2.04%。

Bull/Bear: WinWay Technology (6515.TW)

### Page 6

WinWay Technology (6515.TW)
06 November 2026 Citi Research
6
WinWay Technology
Company description
WinWay, founded in 2001, is a global leader in semiconductor test interfaces
and the world's largest test socket supplier. Originally specializing in high-
speed solutions for IC design and OSAT firms, WinWay now dominates the AI
and HPC sectors. The company provides a comprehensive portfolio including
coaxial sockets, burn-in sockets, probe cards (MEMS & VPC), and advanced

thermal management systems. Its flagship hypersocket technology and
vertically integrated manufacturing enable high-performance validation for
next-generation accelerators. WinWay's diverse solutions support mission-
critical applications across global AI infrastructure, networking, and high-
speed computing.
Investment strategy
We rate WinWay at Buy/High Risk (1H). We believe WinWay is well positioned
for the AI infrastructure cycle, with dominance in the US AI GPU testing

supply chain and participation in US CSP AI ASIC projects. Its leading
proprietary coaxial socket and hypersocket technologies should provide a
defensible technical moat as 224G SerDes and extreme package complexities
expand. The company captures a wider share of the test interface market
through its premium sockets and high-quality service, providing a multi-
layered earnings engine supported by structural growth in AI testing intensity.
Valuation

We value WinWay using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings
momentum and likely expansion of its addressable market. Our target price of
NT$6,500 is derived from applying c.30x to 2H27E-1H28E EPS, in line with
its historical average PE since 2023, which we believe is justified given the
company's entrenched position in the high-end AI GPU testing supply chain,
rising contribution from coaxial socket and SLT solutions, and the increasing
value of test content per module. With these structural drivers in place, and

as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play (rather than a
consumer electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium
relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant rating system rates the WinWay stock High Risk based on its
historical volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price
and our earnings forecasts include: 1) client and sector concentration –
specifically any delays in US AI GPU product roadmap could impact the

business outlook for WinWay; 2) intensifying competition in coaxial and
hypersockets from global peers; 3) faster-than-expected testing algorithm
optimization, which could shorten testing durations and reduce the
anticipated hardware demand multiplier; and 4) delay of MEMS Phase 2 mass
production.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

M
Update
Vanguard International Semiconductor  | Asia
Pacific
Strong demand but margin
headwinds ahead; UW
2027 margin could be under pressure from depreciation: With the ramp up of the
new Singapore fab (VSMC), the company expected  depreciation to increase in 2027,
however, the company did not share the margin dilution guidance. As we noted in a
previous report, we still expect the margin dilution from the Singapore capacity
could be high-single digit for 2027, higher than investors' expectation of low-mid

single digit.
2027 demand remains strong: Despite the slowdown of non-AI demand, the
company expects both 8-inch and 12-inch loading rate to be >90% in 2027 and the
company is under capacity conversion and hopes to keep pushing up the utilization
by capacity optimization. 2027 AI related revenue could also see a significant
increase from interposer production, which could be 20-30% of 2027 revenue, in
our view.
4Q26 revenue could be up mid-single digit QoQ: The company guided 4Q26 wafer

shipments  to be flat to up 2% QoQ given the bottleneck from certain equipment,
but wafer ASP could increase by 3-5% QoQ. Guidance is slightly higher than our 3%
QoQ revenue growth forecast.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347.TWO)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: a cost of equity of 9.2%, a medium-term
growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4.0%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected LCD driver IC demand
n Loosened competition in power semi business
n Faster-than-expected EV penetration
n Inability of IDM customers to move production in-house

Risks to Downside
n Inability to fully utilize VSMC 12-inch investment
n Weaker-than-expected LCD driver IC demand
n Intensified competition in power semi business
n Slower-than-expected EV penetration
n IDM customers moving production in-house

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick_cd366e43.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%) 
ㆍSTRONG BUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +50%이상 3% 
ㆍBUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +15%이상 ~ +50%미만 94% 
ㆍHOLD(중립) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%이상 ∼ +15%미만 3% 
ㆍREDUCE(매도) 추천기준일 종가대비 -10%미만 0% 
(2026.09.30 기준)

3Q26Re: 신규 시설 매출 인식 개시 
연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 6,401억원
(+1.0%, OPM 42.9%)로 컨센서스를 상회하는 실적 기록. 1~4공장이 완전 가동을 유지하는 
한편, 5공장은 PPQ 배치 생산 본격화되면서 매출 기여 확대. 상업 생산 전환이 예상되는 
2H27부터 이익 기여 커질 것으로 기대. 
연초 인수한 Rockville 공장은 3Q에도 안정적인 상업 생산을 이어갔으며 2Q 생산 물량의 
매출이 인식되며 매출 성장에 기여. 동사는 Rockville 공장 매출 인식 등을 반영하여 기존 
+15~20% 성장에서 +20%로 연간 가이던스 상향.   
고객사 수주 회복 기대 
3Q26 기준 누적 수주액은 219억 달러로 전분기 대비 소폭 증가했으나 과거 연간 수주액 
증가 대비 부족한 수준. 저조한 수주 실적은 지난해 초 의약품에 대한 관세 불확실성 증가로 
고객사들의 수주 문의가 저조했던 영향. 26년 초부터 관세 불확실성이 낮아지며 수주 문의
는 회복 추세. 약 1년 정도 논의 기간이 소요되는 것을 고려할 때 26년말~27년 초 수주 회
복 확인 가능할 것으로 기대. 
안정적인 공급망 유지가 중요한 CDMO 산업 특성상 노조 파업 가능성도 고객사 수주를 늦
추는 요인으로 판단. 다만, 10/16 인천 고등법원이 쟁의행위 가처분 항고심에 대해 인용 결
정을 하며 기존 3개 공정에서 9개 공정으로 확대되며 파업 우려는 다소 완화. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원 유지 
매수 의견과 목표 주가 200만원을 유지. 원달러 약세에도 불구하고 5공장 Ramp-up과 
Rockville 공장 매출 기여로 견조한 실적 성장 지속될 것으로 예상. 파업 이슈 해소와 대형 
수주 계약이 주가 상승을 이끌 것으로 판단.

## Document 270: AI監管衝擊與選後美股展望-CTBC20261106

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI監管衝擊與選後美股展望-CTBC20261106.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

AI
監管衝擊與選後美股展望
2026.11.06
美股策略研究
:

厲柏宏

### Page 2

結論與展望
• AI監管風險：民主黨取得國會兩院控制權，AI監管疑慮升溫。2027年資料中心建設進度相對
穩定，但2028年約30GW規劃容量面臨潛在審批風險，恐影響部分專案投產時程。
• 產業影響預期：德州共和黨仍掌握州參議院多數，長期全面禁建面臨立法及司法制衡。過去
約20GW受地方建設限制的容量中，實際延期僅約1.5GW，顯示政策曝險不等於實際損失。
• 政策影響：民主黨可能透過國會制衡財政擴張，中長期若赤字及發債壓力下降，將有利美債
表現。美元預期短空長多，而美伊衝突降溫預期升高，可能使短期油價持續承壓。
• 醫療與零售：民主黨可能爭取恢復部分Medicaid、SNAP及ACA補貼，有助維持中低收入家
庭購買力，支撐消費與醫療需求；惟川普仍掌握關稅行政權，零售業成本改善空間相對有限。
• 美股後市展望：歷史經驗顯示，期中選舉後隨政治不確定性下降，美股通常呈現回升。過去9
次期中選舉後12個月，科技股有8次跑贏大盤，獲利品質、成長及低波動策略亦具相對優勢，
後續仍須關注AI監管落地及企業獲利表現。

1資料來源：中信投顧整理

### Page 3

AI監管提案增加，2028面臨較高審批風險
• 民主黨在期中選舉取得參眾兩院 ，並提出超級 AI禁令及資料中心暫停建設法案，分別涉及限
制AI模型開發，及暫停新建資料中心直至相關機制建立 ，引發對監管趨嚴與投資放緩的擔憂 。
• SemiAnalysis估計，美國2027預計交付的資料中心約有75%已動工，短期進度穩定；惟2028，
規劃多數尚未施工，若暫停建設政策擴大，約30GW新增容量可能面臨審批延後風險。
2資料來源：dcmap.us、中信投顧整理
AI監管風險
美國各州資料中心容量分布與建設進度 (GW)

### Page 4

州級監管受立法制衡，政策曝險不等於實際損失
• 州長行政權仍受州議會及司法制衡 ，以德州為例，共和黨仍掌握州參議院過半席次，全面禁
建制度化仍需跨黨派支持；惟州政府可延續部分許可限制，短期專案進度仍可能受影響。
• 過去一年約20GW受地方建設限制的容量中，實際延誤僅約 1.5GW；預期監管趨嚴將提高開
發成本並延後部分2028專案，短期影響AI硬體訂單與交付，但長期算力需求未必減少。
3資料來源：MultiState Elctions、SemiAnalysis、中信投顧整理
產業影響預期
11/05德州期中選舉統計 地方禁令實際影響有限

### Page 5

國會制衡與美伊局勢緩和，牽動美債及美元走勢
• 民主黨掌控國會後，較可能透過立法及預算權制衡共和黨政策 ，降低進一步財政擴張空間。
短期政策不確定性可能加大債市波動 ，但中長期若赤字與發債壓力下降，將有利美債表現。
而AI投資貢獻美國經濟成長比例加大，故美元表現將與AI股趨勢相同，預期美元短空長多。
• 伊朗戰爭推升汽油價格成為共和黨選舉壓力來源 ，隨民主黨掌控國會提高延續軍事行動的政
治成本，加上美伊持續透過中東國家間接接觸，市場對衝突降溫預期升高，油價短期回落。
4資料來源：中信投顧整理
政策影響
美國總統與國會權力

### Page 6

國會控制權轉向，醫療與消費政策迎來調整空間
• 民主黨可能透過債務上限協商，爭取延後或縮減 Medicaid、SNAP減支，並恢復ACA增額保
費補貼，有助維持中低收入家庭購買力與醫療保險覆蓋率，對民生消費、醫療服務偏正面。
• 關稅也是本次輪替重點，但川普仍握有貿易政策行政權與301條款等工具，整體有效關稅稅率
未必明顯下降；因此零售業獲利改善仍取決於企業定價能力及供應鏈調整成效。
5資料來源：Commonwealth Fund、Uni. of Penn、中信投顧整理
醫療與零售
現行法規預期刪減福利 美國現行關稅預估

### Page 7

期中選舉後政治不確定性下降，歷史上美股通常回升
• 觀察過去數次美國期中選舉，美股通常在選前因政治不確定性升高而面臨較大波動 ，但選後
隨著政策方向逐漸明朗，期中選舉後標普多數呈現上漲趨勢。
• 產業方面，科技股在過去9次期中選舉後的12個月，科技股有8次跑贏標普。平均報酬最高的
選股策略則為獲利品質、成長與低波動類股。
6資料來源：JPM、Barclays、中信投顧整理

## Document 271: AI関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか ～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか ～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年10 月 26 日

1/6 
AI 関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか 
～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ 世界的な AI ブームが続くなか、AI 関連企業がデータセンター建設などの巨額投資を賄うた
めに社債発行や借入による資金調達を急増させている。「AI関連企業は手元資金が潤沢で財
務が盤石だ」という従来の常識は揺らぎつつあり、企業の資金不足や信用リスクに目を向け
る必要がある。 
○ AI 投資の拡大は3 つの変化を通じて、企業の財務と金融市場にリスクをもたらしうる。第一
に、テクノロジー企業の社債発行が急増し、借入依存が強まっている。第二に、巨額の設備
投資がAI大手の現金創出力（フリーキャッシュフロー）を圧迫している。第三に、信用力の
低いテクノロジー企業に対する社債市場の警戒が強まり、信用スプレッドが拡大している。 
○ 「AIの成長期待さえあれば巨額の投資もすべて回収できる」と安易に想定すべきではない。
AI 投資の資金を借入などの外部資金に頼る度合いが高まる環境では、収益化の遅れが企業の
信用力低下に直結しやすく、従来の資産配分についても再点検が必要となる。 
○ 個人投資家にとっては、保有する社債ファンドの業種偏りや信用リスクを点検することと、
株式運用において特定の AI 銘柄への過度な集中を避け、安定したキャッシュフローを生む
資産を補完的に組み入れることが、対応策の一つとなるだろう。

1. 「キャッシュリッチなAI企業」の変調 
生成AIの普及を原動力とする世界的なハイテク株高は、日本の個人投資家にとっても資産形成
の大きな追い風となってきた。NISA（少額投資非課税制度）などを通じて米国株式（S&P500な
ど）や全世界株式を保有する投資家の多くは、AI関連企業の成長や株価上昇の恩恵を受けてきた
といえる。もっとも、足元ではAI関連株の値動きが荒くなる場面も増え、巨額の投資が本当に利
益につながるのか不安を感じている投資家も少なくないだろう。一方で、市場には依然として「A
I企業の財務は盤石であり、足元の揺らぎは一時的な調整にすぎない」とみる楽観論も根強い。し
かし、企業の「台所事情」に目を向けると、これまでとは異なるリスクが見えてくる。 「AIブー
ム」と聞くと、華やかな技術革新や株価の上昇ばかりに目が向きがちだが、企業財務の視点に立
つと、巨額のデータセンター投資が企業の手元資金を圧迫し、一部のAI関連企業では借入などの

### Page 2

2/6 
外部資金への依存度が高まりつつある。 
華やかなAI株高の裏側で膨らむ巨額の借金を、AI関連企業は本当に返せるのだろうか。 「テクノ
ロジー企業は潤沢な現金を持ち、財務が健全だ」という従来の常識にとらわれていると、資金不
足や信用リスクの拡大という構造的な変化を見落としかねない。本稿では、AI投資の拡大が引き
起こす 3つの変化として、 「社債発行の急増」 「現金創出力の圧迫」 「信用スプレッドの拡大」を整
理し、個人投資家が資産運用において注意すべき論点を提示したい。なお、本稿では、AI投資を
主導する巨大テクノロジー企業（ハイパースケーラー）から、AI関連事業を手掛ける中堅企業ま
でを幅広く「AI関連企業」と総称する。また、 「テクノロジー企業」は、社債発行統計などにおい
てテクノロジー（IT）部門に分類される企業を指す用語として用いる。

2. AI投資の拡大はなぜ資金不足と信用リスクを招くのか 
(1) AI投資の拡大で急増するテクノロジー企業の社債発行 
現在のAIブームを牽引する巨大テクノロジー企業（ハイパースケーラー）をはじめとするAI関
連企業は、データセンターの建設や先端半導体の確保、電力インフラの整備などに巨額の資金を
投じている。これまでテクノロジー企業は、株式発行や潤沢な手元資金を主な資金源としてきた
が、投資規模の急拡大に伴い、資金調達のあり方が大きく変わりつつある。 
RBA（豪州準備銀行）が2026 年10月に公表した「Financial Stability Review」によれば、AI
関連企業は大規模な投資計画を賄うため、社債やプライベート・クレジット、銀行借入など、負
債による資金調達への依存を強めている。資料1の上段は米国の投資適格社債の発行総額に占め
るテクノロジー部門の割合を、下段はハイパースケーラー（巨大クラウド・AI企業）とテクノロ
ジー部門の実際の社債発行額を示したものである。2026年には、米国の投資適格社債の発行総額
に占めるテクノロジー部門の割合が約27％に達している。また、下段が示すように、足元ではハ
イパースケーラーによる起債が急増し、部門全体の社債発行額を大きく押し上げている。RBAは、
今後計画されているAI関連の設備投資のうち3分の1以上が負債性資金によって賄われるとの市
場の見方を紹介している。つまり、AIブームが続くほど、テクノロジー企業の借入依存が強ま
り、公募・私募の債券市場全体がAI投資の成否に左右されやすくなるという新たな構図が生まれ
ている。

### Page 3

3/6 
資料 1 テクノロジー部門の社債発行が急増、米国投資適格社債の発行総額に占める割合は約 27％に 
（注）米国の非金融企業による投資適格社債の発行額（期間は 2010 年 3 月～2026 年 7 月 17 日）。テクノロジー部門にはコンピ
ュータ・電子機器を含む。上段の割合は 4 四半期移動平均。ハイパースケーラーは Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、
Oracle、NVIDIA を指す。 
（出所）Reserve Bank of Australia, “Financial Stability Review – October 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) 巨額の設備投資が企業の現金創出力を圧迫 
なぜ、高い収益力を誇る巨大テクノロジー企業までもが借入への依存を強めているのか。その
背景には、データセンター構築などの設備投資があまりにも巨額であり、本業の稼ぐ力（営業利
益）だけでは投資資金を賄いきれなくなっている現実がある。 
IMF（国際通貨基金）が2026 年10月に公表した「Global Financial Stability Report」は、
AI投資の中心を担う主要ハイパースケーラー5社（Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、
Oracle）の財務状況を分析している。資料2は、これら 5社の設備投資（Capex：Capital 
Expenditure） 、営業利益、およびフリーキャッシュフローの推移を示したものである。5社の設備
投資は 2023年頃から急拡大し、2025年後半から2026年にかけて営業利益を上回る規模に達して
いる。その結果、フリーキャッシュフロー（右軸）は急減し、2023～24年のピーク時には四半期
ベースで600 億米ドルを超えていたものが、2026年にはほぼゼロまで落ち込んでいる。もっと
も、ハイパースケーラー各社は現時点では強固なバランスシートと十分な流動性を維持してい
る。しかし、激しい競争圧力や技術の陳腐化に対応するため、企業が投資の手を緩めることは難
しい。今後、AIの収益化が市場の期待を下回るようなことがあれば、企業の現金創出力は一段と
圧迫され、追加の資金調達や財務負担の増大を招く可能性がある。

### Page 4

4/6

資料 2 主要ハイパースケーラー5 社の設備投資が急増し、2026 年のフリーキャッシュフローはほぼゼロに 
（注）主要ハイパースケーラー5 社（Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、Oracle）の設備投資（Capex、左軸）、営業利益（左軸）、
およびフリーキャッシュフロー（右軸）の四半期ごとの推移（単位：10 億米ドル）。 
（出所）IMF, “Global Financial Stability Report, October 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) 信用力の低いテクノロジー企業に対する社債市場の警戒 
さらに、AI投資への借入依存が高まるなか、社債市場ではテクノロジー企業の信用リスクに対
する警戒が強まりつつある。株式市場ではAIの成長期待が注目されやすいが、元利金の確実な返
済を重視する債券市場では、企業の財務体力やビジネスモデルの持続可能性に対する選別がシビ
アに進んでいる。 
ECB（欧州中央銀行）が2026 年5月に公表した「Financial Stability Review」では、AIを巡
る懸念が株式市場だけでなく社債市場にも波及していると指摘している。資料3 は、2025年 3月
から 2026年5 月にかけてのユーロ圏および米国におけるハイイールド（HY、低格付け）社債の信
用スプレッドの推移を示したものである。米国・ユーロ圏ともに、ハイイールド債市場全体（米
国HY、ユーロ圏HY）のスプレッドが300ベーシスポイント（bp）前後で比較的落ち着いて推移し
ていたのに対し、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業（米国HYテック、ユーロ圏HYテック）のスプレ
ッドは 2026年に入り500bp超へと大きく拡大し、同年 5月時点でも450bp前後と、市場全体を上
回る水準で推移していた。 
ECBは、AIが既存のソフトウェア企業などのビジネスモデルを脅かすリスクに加え、内部資金だ
けでなく借入に頼ってAI投資を拡大させる動きが金融安定上の懸念になりうると指摘している。
巨額のAI投資ブームの裏側では、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業に対する市場の警戒が強まり、
企業の借入依存が信用市場にも新たなリスクをもたらしつつある。 「借りた巨額の資金を本当に返

### Page 5

5/6 
せるのか」という市場の問いかけがすでに始まっているのである。

資料 3 信用力の低いテクノロジー企業の信用スプレッドはハイイールド債市場全体より大きく拡大 
（注）2025 年 3 月 1 日～2026 年 5 月 19 日におけるユーロ圏および米国のハイイールド（HY）社債の信用スプレッド（OIS カーブ 
に対する上乗せ金利、時価総額加重平均、単位：ベーシスポイント）。「HY テック」はハイイールド格付けのテクノロジー企業
を指す。 
縦の破線は左から 2025 年 4 月の米国の関税発表、前回 FSR（金融安定報告書）公表時点（2025 年 11 月）、中東戦争の開 
始（2026 年 3 月初旬）を示す。 
（出所）European Central Bank, “Financial Stability Review, May 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

3. 「AI借金時代」の資産防衛術 
第 2 章で見てきたように、AI ブームの裏側では「社債発行の急増」 「現金創出力の圧迫」 「信用
スプレッドの拡大」という3 つの変化が同時に進行している。この視点に立てば、AIブームが抱
える死角は「株価の割高感」だけでは説明できない。巨大テクノロジー企業から中堅のテクノロ
ジー企業に至るまで、AI投資の拡大に伴い、一部の企業では自己資金だけでなく、借入などの外
部資金への依存度が高まりつつある。 
ここで重要なのは、 「テクノロジー企業はキャッシュリッチで財務が盤石だ」と安易に考えない
ことである。仮にAI の収益化が遅れれば、膨らんだ借入負担が企業の業績や信用力を直撃するシ
ナリオも視野に入れる必要がある。こうした「AI借金時代」を迎えるなか、個人投資家には、従
来のポートフォリオに潜む信用リスクを改めて点検することが求められる。

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
第一に、社債ファンドやハイイールド債（低格付け社債）における「信用リスク」を過小評価
しないことである。2026年の米国の投資適格社債の発行総額に占めるテクノロジー部門の割合が
約27％に達し、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業のスプレッドが拡大している現状では、 「債券だか
ら安心」 「株安を社債がカバーしてくれる」という従来の前提が働きにくい可能性がある。AI の収
益期待が後退する局面では、AI 関連株の下落と信用スプレッドの拡大（社債価格の下落）が同時
に進みかねない点に注意が必要だ。債券を保有する場合には、表面的な利回りの高さだけでハイ
イールド債や社債ファンドを選好するのではなく、組入資産の業種の偏りを確認することや、信
用リスクの低い先進国国債などへの分散を検討することも、選択肢の一つとなる。 
第二に、株式運用において「AI関連だから買う」という一括りの発想から脱却し、企業の現金
創出力と業種分散を重視することである。NISAなどを活用してS&P500や全世界株式連動商品を保
有する投資家は多いが、これらの指数では、米国の巨大テクノロジー企業の構成比率が高まって
おり、特にS&P500では一部の企業への集中が進んでいる。指数連動商品をポートフォリオの中核
に据えつつも、補完的な資産として、安定したフリーキャッシュフローを生み出す高配当バリュ
ー株やディフェンシブ株、あるいはAIデータセンター投資の受け皿となる電力・インフラ関連株
などを組み入れることは、AI 企業の資金不足や淘汰の波に備える選択肢の一つとなるだろう。た
だし、特定のテーマや業種に偏りすぎないよう、投資比率には十分な注意が必要である。 
AI 革命は株式市場に大きな成長機会をもたらす一方で、企業の財務構造や信用市場のリスクを
も変えつつある。これからは、 「AIだから成長する」という発想にとどまらず、その裏側で膨らむ
「借金の行方」まで見据えることが重要になる。AI 企業のフリーキャッシュフローや信用スプレ
ッドの動きも、株価と同様に注視する。それが、巨額のAI投資時代において投資家に求められる
新たな視点であろう。

以 上

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As expected, the RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) lifted
the cash rate 25bps to 4.85% at its November meeting.
This follows the increase at the previous (late-September)
meeting and is a marked turnaround from the outlook back
in August. The post-meeting media statement cited higher

global energy prices resulting from the Middle East conflict,
tighter domestic capacity pressures and an increase in
inflation expectations for the decision.
However, the decision was a split vote, 6 votes to 3, a
possibility we have flagged for some time. In the post-meeting
media conference, Governor Bullock explained that the
dissenting minority put more weight on the downside risks and
did not see signs of wages growth picking up as much as the

staff forecasts implied. Those members were reportedly more
confident that slower growth would prevent higher inflation
expectations becoming entrenched.
Financial conditions are restrictive and this is expected to
bring inflation down over time. Because the RBA assesses the
risks to the inflation outlook to still be skewed to the upside,
the outlook for coming meetings will still be for a decision
between holding and hiking. But we note that the post-meeting

statement omitted September’s language “including increasing
the cash rate target further if needed” from the commitment to
do what is necessary to get inflation down.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor
commented that after five hikes, this is probably a good time
to “take stock”. Together with the dissents, we read this as
confirming our current view that the MPB will keep rates
on hold in December. There is also considerable data flow

between now and then, which could go either way, including
the Q3 national accounts.
Similarly, a lot could happen between now and February, but
we see it as the next most likely timing for a hike, should one
eventuate. We still see that as a risk rather than a base case.
Revised forecasts published in the accompanying Statement
on Monetary Policy (SMP) show underlying inflation lingering
around current rates for longer. This contrasts with the
previous SMP forecasts in August, which showed trimmed

mean inflation returning to the 2–3%yr target range by late
2027 and a little below the 2.5%yr target midpoint in 2028.
We note, however, that the conditioning assumptions on oil
prices, which are based on futures prices, were at a local peak
at the time the forecasts were finalised.
RBA hikes cash rate to new high of 4.85%, a December pause likely
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The RBA’s GDP growth forecasts for 2027 and 2028 were
both revised down –0.3%pts to 1.3% for 2027 and 1.5% in

2028, despite an upward revision to the near term. This is
well below even the RBA’s downbeat view of potential output
growth. Within the GDP aggregate, recent misses and leading
indicators have required upward revisions to the near-term
outlooks for business and dwelling investment, and downside
revisions to public demand. These revisions were offset further
out with downward revisions to the 2028 outlook for business
and dwelling investment and an upward revision to public

demand. Household consumption forecasts were revised down
across most of the profile.
With the unemployment rate already at 4.7% in August and
September – a rate the RBA did not forecast until end-2027 in
its August SMP forecasts – the RBA has lifted its unemployment
forecast to show a peak of 5.1% in at end-2028, the end of the
forecast horizon. This largely reflects stronger labour supply,
with forecasts for employment growth essentially unchanged.

We have repeatedly flagged that cost-of-living pressures are
likely to keep people in the labour force and thus boost the
participation rate. The RBA had previously not factored this
adequately into its forecasts but acknowledged in the SMP
that this mechanism had kept participation higher than it had
expected at the time of the August SMP.
However, because inflation surprised to the upside recently,
this additional labour supply has had no bearing on the

RBA’s assessment of capacity pressures. The RBA assesses
the domestic economy to still be a little tight, and tighter
than previously judged. The RBA’s framework for assessing
the degree of spare capacity or tightness in the economy
necessarily translates an upside surprise on inflation (or unit
labour costs) into a conclusion that the economy must be
tighter than previously judged. Indeed, the RBA’s estimate
of the NAIRU (unemployment rate consistent with inflation

stable at target) has been revised up to 4.75%.
The RBA highlighted that elevated inflation expectations may
be contributing to higher realised inflation. Consumer inflation
expectations have started to lift again but remain below where
they peaked earlier this year. However, the RBA appears
“RBA lifts cash rate 25bps to 4.85% in split
vote, citing “broadly based” domestic inflation
pressures, Middle East conflict and AI boom."

to be putting more attention on the inflation expectations
of firms and the frequency of mentions of pricing in their
liaison program. This is putting a lot of weight on proprietary
information that is not clearly validated by other, longer-

The AI boom is heavily cited in the SMP and other post-
meeting communication. The RBA has lifted its forecasts
for major trading partners, especially high-income Asian
economies that are the centre of high-tech manufacturing.
Demand for AI-related goods is also cited as a driver of both
global and domestic inflation. And although the RBA has
revised up its near-term forecasts for business investment
on the back of strong data centre activity, it is working on

the assumption that Australia does not become ‘a significant
regional hub for data centres’. Nor does it allow for any boost
to productivity trends from the technology, even in 2028. This
is a very different view of the risks from AI than the RBNZ
expressed last week. For the RBA, it is all about inflation, a
little bit about pressure on domestic construction resources,
and nothing about either an ugly unwind of the current
financing boom or a future productivity dividend.

THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: whiplash
total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of
Australia’s data-centre investment boom, which should feature
prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3

Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term

elections. In the House, Senate and a series of State Governor
races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come
2027, the Democrats will have a comfortable majority in both
houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-
of-living pressures. The election result also limits President
Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against
Iran. Notably, both immediately before and after the election,

President Trump and the administration have exhibited a greater
interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not
a surprise that Brent oil has backed off its most recent highs
around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to
near USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since
traded back up to above USD99, still 16% below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a
robust broad-based rally in major equity indexes, concerns

over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related
infrastructure buildout jolted US equities lower overnight – the
Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of
the prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and
FOMC rate expectations have also been scaled back; so, while
tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better
outcomes across the rest of the US economy may become
evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest

their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in
the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s concerns over US inflation
should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not
hike in December is a high bar, but the probability of further
hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate
by 25bps to 4.00% at their November meeting as expected.
On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to

rise from 3.5%yr at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity
data has also surprised to the upside in recent months, though
"there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other
prices and wages".
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase
at its November meeting, taking the cash rate to 4.85%. The
move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued
concern around inflation following the recent run higher in
global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation
is now not expected to return to the mid-point of the
target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth
expectations and the unemployment rate trending up to
5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with
three members preferring to remain on hold given downside
risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth.
Governor Bullock’s suggestion that it was time to “take stock”

after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference
to raising rates further “if needed”. In our view, February
represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but
we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible.
Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in October to now
be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline
is approaching the scale of previous housing corrections but

appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals  saw a 5.1% headline gain in
September, but this was driven by volatile unit approvals while
detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates
and the established-market downturn are beginning to weigh
on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending  meanwhile
rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3; however, much of
the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly

for fuel. Real spending volumes expanded by a much more
modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at
income, spending and saving flows. The latest update suggests
that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are
increasingly being squeezed, particularly those with low savings
buffers. With another rate increase now delivered, housing
wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like

the high-water mark for growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus
widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-monetary gold trade
yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The
most notable development was another exceptionally strong
result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the Q3

In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the
nascent improvement in energy price pressures over the

also conveyed that "Unless progress towards resolving the
conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk

to inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may

was a decision that needs to be made in December, but rather
a risk to be judged as additional information is received. This
points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
No deals are done
This week’s developments underscore our message that
a rate hike at the RBNZ’s December meeting is far from a
done deal. New Zealand’s labour market remains softer
than expected, and that will be noted by those on the

policy committee who question the extent of second-round
inflation pressures. The rout in the US mid-term elections
has also taken significant heat out of oil prices for now,
although it’s unclear whether that situation will hold through
to next month. Our view on the endpoint for this OCR cycle
remains unchanged, but there’s a growing risk that the RBNZ
could slow the pace of tightening in the new year.
The September quarter labour market surveys pointed to

ongoing softness in the jobs market. The unemployment
rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing a four-year run
of increases, and reaching its highest level since 2015.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth
remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-living pressures.
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise
0.1% fall in employment in the September quarter, after a
downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That

went against the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure
of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward momentum
emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure
had outpaced the MEI for the previous nine straight quarters).
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in
the labour force participation rate from 70.6% to 70.4%.
These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly
large increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had

suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error
in the survey rather than a genuine surge.
We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate,
which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message
here is one of softness. Our best interpretation is that, to the
extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the
growth in the population (which itself is a fairly low bar, as net
migration remains at a low ebb).

Even with the economy returning to growth over the last
year or so, hiring activity has remained weak. This may be
an ongoing legacy of employment trends in previous years.
Businesses have told us that they felt burned by the severe
skill shortages that developed over 2021-23, and looked to
hold on to workers to the extent they could during the 2024-
25 downturn. The flipside of this is that they may now find
themselves overstaffed even as activity picks up again.

This shows up most strikingly in labour force transition rates.
The rise in unemployment over the last few years has not
been driven by an increase in job losses, but by accumulation
of people who, once out of work, are finding it increasingly
difficult to get back in again. That also extends to young
people trying to enter the workforce for the first time – youth
unemployment of around 25% is the highest in the history of
the survey going back to 1986.

The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September
quarter, in line with our expectations overall but a bit weaker
in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while
there was a surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector
wages. There was a notable jump in the number of employers
citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay
rates – although the size of those increases varied a great deal,
with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate.

Potential RBNZ implications
The labour market data was unequivocally weaker than
expected by either us or the RBNZ. While likely not a
gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the
arguments of the doves on the Monetary Policy Committee that
a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon – perhaps in
December or early next year. It’s likely the case that interest rate
markets were over-pricing the odds of a hike at the 9 December

MPS, so the subsequent pullback in pricing makes sense.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting was to
be careful about pricing in "done deals", and that the outlook
Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside
measures of inflation expectations, the key domestic data
to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the
output gap), as this will be a key determinant of the Bank’s

assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
The RBNZ looks at a broad suite of indicators of labour market
slack, although their research found that only four of them
seem to have predictive power: the unemployment rate,
the vacancy to unemployment ratio, the QSBO measure of
labour as the main limiting factor to growth, and the rate of
transition between jobs. The first three of these all pointed
to a softening in the September quarter. (The fourth one is

calculated from income tax data, so it’s not publicly available
and even the RBNZ receives it only with a lag.)
In the eyes of the dovish MPC members, the softness in
the labour market indicators will likely push in the direction
of having greater comfort with the medium-term inflation
outlook. The outcome of the election, with the possibility of a
return to a dual inflation/employment mandate, could increase
the prominence of these factors. This likely won’t be an explicit

factor in the RBNZ’s reasoning at its December review, but it
may be in the minds of individual MPC members.
That said, it’s important to remember that in the RBNZ’s
forecasting framework, the GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy. The most
recent GDP data tells us that the output gap probably
narrowed a bit through the June quarter, mainly reflecting
historical revisions. In light of the latest labour market figures,

there may be some downside risk to the outlook for Q3 GDP –
but there are conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear.
At last month’s surprise on-hold OCR decision, Governor
Breman lamented that there was no protocol for the RBNZ
to be able to signal how it was reading the economic data –
in particular the softer than expected Q3 CPI print – ahead
of the policy meeting. In that light, it will be interesting to
see whether they use some already-scheduled speaking

engagements to signal what they make of the labour market
data. Top of mind is Chief Economist Conway's 10 November
speech to the UBS Australasia Conference (pointedly titled
“How is the NZ economy tracking”). Governor Breman is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November.
Our view remains that the December OCR hike is more likely
than not. But there will be a clear debate to be had between
the MPC hawks and doves, and it may again come down to a

vote. We still expect a 4% peak in the OCR for this cycle, but
the time taken to get there could be more flexible. Takes along
the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" seem rather hasty on the
basis of a single data release.
Aside from how the Committee interprets the labour market
data, the key considerations in December will be: the RBNZ’s
surveys of inflation expectations by households and businesses;
whether the US mid-term elections lead to a sustained de-

escalation in the Middle East and a pullback in oil prices; and
whether developments in the AI space exacerbate some MPC
members’ concerns about the global economic environment.
And then of course there’s the result of the General Election,
with voting closing on Saturday evening. The actual formation
of a government is likely to take weeks of negotiation, so the
outcome may not be known by the 9 December MPS but at least
the range of possibilities should be clearer. We’ll publish our

initial thoughts on the election results early on Monday morning.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Oct Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Nov 12, Last: –0.4, Westpac f/c: 1.4
We’re forecasting a 1.4% rise in retail spending in October.
While that would be a solid increase, it’s in large part due to
the 11% rise in petrol prices over the past month. Stripping out

fuel spending, we’re forecasting a 0.6% rise in core spending
categories. Core spending has been very choppy recently,
and the increase we’re assuming follows falls in the past few
months. A result in line with our expectations would leave the
trend in core spending relatively flat since the start of this year
with the rise in fuel prices weighing on spending in other areas.
AUS: Nov Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Nov 10, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment fell 4.7% to 80.4 in Oct, returning to the

deeply pessimistic levels seen earlier in the year. Responses
over the course of the survey week showed a very sharp
deterioration after the RBA rate hike.
The Nov survey is in the field over the week ended Nov 7.
Interest rates will again factor with the RBA raising the cash
rate by another 25bps to 4.85%, an 18yr high. Average pump
prices also pushed higher, the national average just under
$2.40/litre, while the housing market downturn has continued

to broaden and deepen. The US mid-term election results may
have some effects on sentiment locally as well.
AUS: Q3 Housing Finance Approvals (%qtr)
Nov 11 Last: –5.2, Westpac f/c: –10
Market f/c: –8.5, Range: –15 to –5
The total value of housing finance approvals fell The value of
new dwelling finance approvals excl. refinance fell 5.2%qtr,
the largest quarterly drop in three years. Annual growth eased
to 7.3%yr. The weakness was accounted for mainly by lower

lending volumes: loan numbers were down 4.0%qtr (–0.6%yr),
while average loan size also contributed negatively.
Q3 is expected to see an even bigger decline with the full
impact of interest rate rises and tax policy changes showing
through. The value of both loan enquiries and turnover fell by
around 10% in the quarter. We expect total finance approvals
to show a 10% drop as well, with investor loan approvals likely
to be down 16%qtr and owner occupier approvals down 6%qtr.

### Page 9

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
9
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
US: Oct CPI (%mth)
Nov 11, Headline, Last: 0.6, Market f/c: 0.3, Westpac f/c: 0.4
US consumer prices rose by 0.6% in September, although
half of the gain was due to energy inflation, with core prices
instead up 0.3%. Over the year, prices were reported to have

risen 3.7%yr overall and 2.6%yr excluding food and energy.
Constructively, ex food, energy and shelter, annual inflation
met the FOMC’s medium-term target of 2.0%yr.
Come October, energy price pressures are set to recede
given the pre-election run higher in oil prices came late in the
month. A softer print for shelter is also likely to moderate core
momentum. We see a 0.4% gain for the headline measure and
a 0.2% result for core inflation as most probable. Looking to

2027, disinflation is expected to continue at a measured pace
towards the medium-term target.

in the United Kingdom) (“MAR”). In accordance with the relevant

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn). 
Kháng cự xa: 1820 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1775 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1670 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

衷方 案， 一度 增 添 市場對 兩 方談 判突 破預
期，然雙方對 核問題 意見仍分歧 ，疊加美以
雙方準備進行軍事備戰 ，恐於期中選後加大
對伊朗打擊 ，加劇中東局勢不確定性與市場
擾動。市場持續聚焦美國企業 財報季表現 ，
金融業財 報良好開局形成 股價下方支撐 ，而
賽默飛世爾 科技等醫療企業營收 、獲利與展

望皆高於預期，推動醫療類股漲勢。

半導體與科技類股表現顯弱 。AI巨頭財報由
Meta平台打頭陣 ，AI驅動廣告 效率與營收與
展望 優 於 預期 並 上調全 年 資 本支 出 下 緣。
SpaceX獲利由虧轉盈且優於市場共識為股價
添柴火，Tesla財報好壞參半，短期內飛天跑
車貢獻不大 ，後續聚焦潛在合併所面臨的地
緣風險。通訊企業財報方面，儘管AT&T與網
飛財報優於預期 ，但面對衛星通訊 競爭疑慮
及與第四季展望低於預期 ，加深通訊服務類
股下跌壓力。

► 美國財政部 20年期公債拍賣結果顯示需求低
於預期 ，財政部標售 20年期公債共 130億美
元，最高得標殖利率達 5.469%，為1986年1
月以來20年期公債拍賣最高利率 ，長天期美
債需求仍疲 。信用債市面對 AI大額發債與資
金回收效率擔憂 ，信用債利差一度擴大 ，隨
企業財報揭幕 ，市場資金流入具防禦性質的
高信評債種 ，如美國投資級債與 亞洲投資級
債表現相對抗跌。
► 美伊衝突可能進一步擴大 ，美國公布截至上
周原油庫存減少 310萬桶 ，降幅高於市場預
期，地緣風險難消與原油供需失衡 壓力，油
價震盪上行 。加密貨幣市場亦見反彈 ，美國
貨幣監理署 (OCC)發布公告敲 定GENIUS法
案最終實施細則 ，疊加近期機構投資者對加
密貨幣接受度 提升推動近期 漲勢。匯市主要
受到美伊戰事添通膨變數 ，投資人靜 待下週
聯準會利率決策結果，美元相對承壓。

► 今年以來適 逢美伊衝突難題遲遲未解 ，高油
價讓聯準會重啟升息且堆高升息 預期，面對
部分聯準會官員保持觀望立場 ，利率期貨顯
示聯準會將升息一碼 且時點可能在 12月，未
來1年則存在3碼潛在升息空間 ，考量地緣衝
突短期 內持 續 ， 若 通膨難 以改善 至政 策目
標，預期聯準會2027年將維持緊縮貨幣政策
的鷹派立場來對抗通膨，然美國2年期公債利
率已多反映 未來升息預期 ，後續仍視美國核
心物價發展，引導短天期公債利率走向。
► 儘管全球利率大幅上升 ，然對於整體 金融環
境影響 甚微，反映美 股和信用 市場低波動狀
態，體現實質 政策利率低於 緊縮利率 區間與
彭博金 融情勢指數變化不大 ，當前美國貨幣
政策與市場流動性仍偏寬鬆 ，倘若地緣擾動

持續造成通 膨居高難下 ，恐進一步推升聯準
會升息預期 ，疊加政府財政 風險與貨幣政策
透明度降 ，加劇長 天期公債利率 上行風險 ，
長天期債以交易為主，不建議長期持有。

► 特斯拉3Q26財報好壞參半 — 毛利率與交付
量超預 期 ， 營收 大 致符合 ，營業 利潤 遜預
期，最大意外是自由現金流達 15億美元，高
於共識負43億，資本開支指引下調成憂慮。
► 早前公司展示下代 Roadster能力，本質上是
光環車 ，定價高受眾少 ，2028 年才有望投
產，盈利貢獻有限 。Model 3/Y成為大眾商
品，競爭激烈 ，利潤率壓縮 。正如傳統品牌
以賽道或參賽展示技術 ，「飛天」旨在展示
獨有跨界整合能力，維持品牌形象溢價。
► 至於和SpaceX的潛在合併，核心在於貫通多
頂 業 務， 提 高 資 本 運 用 效 率， 共同分擔
Terafab 的開支等 ，現時兩家合計市值約為
3.5兆美元。這項巨型潛在交易 ，除了要跨過
極高表決門檻外，SpaceX具極敏感的美國國
防及NASA合作背景 ，而特斯拉有超過 20%
業務倚重上海超級工廠 ，合併存在嚴重地緣
衝突。預期馬斯克將在川普離任前 ，加速執
行Tesla與SpaceX 合併。

投資焦點
► 今年9月適逢地緣擾動持續推升高油價、聯準
會重啟 升息 、財政部 與企 業發債 浪潮 等 影
響，形塑債市今年以來單月最差表現 。檢視
2021年以來美國投資級債前十大單月最差總
報酬，2022年聯準會 3月啟動升息年度就佔
一半以上，顯示利率風險仍為近5年來債市最
大風險 ，然檢視單月最差報酬表現未來期間
總報酬，除2022年1月和3月聯準會重啟升息
前夕與當月表現明顯較弱，多數於未來1年後
多呈現正報酬 ，反映隨利率走升放大孳息效
益，同時挹注債券長期持有之報酬空間。
► 今年來債券市場利率波動明顯推升信用債利
差，其 中僅 以低 信 評債利 差升幅 較顯 ，此
外，9月以來利率連動度高及AI相關產業債種
也成為利差擴大重災區 ，凸顯債市投資人減
輕市場不確定性的投資傾向 ，考量信用債市
歷經利率上行利空洗禮 ，信用利差同步擴大

創造收益空間 ，可把握債信體質仍佳企業債
鎖利良機，並透過時間複利力量強化收益。
利率波動升，僅低信評利差擴大程度相對明顯9月為美國投資級債今年最差單月報酬
用時間換空間，單月最差表現形塑未來報酬機會

### Page 8

8
► 受聯準會重啟升息影響 ，過去一年美國公債
殖利率曲線持續陡峭化 ，其中利率最大升幅
落在短天期的 2至3年期間，美國核心通膨與
就業仍具韌性 ，聯準會將持續保持 緊縮貨幣
政策步調來應對高通膨 ，考量美國高物價與
高利率環境短期 難消，宜透過縮短債券配置
天期並進行動態調整 ，為因應後續利率不確
定性環境的良策。
► 為因應利率不確定性 ，投資人可採取債券梯
的投資配置 ，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行 組合，債券梯投資重點主要在
於，根據殖利率曲線型態及利率動態建立不
同期間 債券梯，假設市場利率走升且殖利率
曲線正斜率 ，建立短天期債券梯除了降低利
率風險 ，有 短期 閒 置資金 及孳息 需求 投資
人，亦可取得穩定孳息 收益。若後續投資人
有短期資金需求 ，可以視情況將短期債券到
期後資金回收 ，降低只投資單一債券須持至
到期或直接變現的流動性風險。
投資焦點
美國公債殖利率曲線持續為正斜率
高利率波動持續，用策略迎戰市場擾動
資料來源：Bloomberg，凱基證券整理

建立短天期債券梯，分散風險及穩定現金流
債券梯
經過第2年第一檔 債券到期，視資金需求 及市況與利率 動
態決定後續配置

短天期債券梯 ：假設買入 2檔各 3、4、5及7年到期債券 ，
聚焦 短期穩定收益
► 債券梯是一種分散投資方式 ，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行組合，可兼具：
1) 分散利率風險： 將資金分別買進不同到期年期的債券 ，以分
散利率風險。
2) 分散信用風險： 分散投資不同發行 機構債券，不會重壓 單一
債券並可分散信用違約風險。
3) 降低流動性風險： 若投資人有短期資金需求 ，可以視情況將
短期債券到期後資金回收 ，降低只投資單一債券須持至到期
或直接變現之流動性風險。

### Page 9

9
股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長 ，市場靜待財報結果與展望 ，和對AI資本支出回收影響 。
考量地緣衝突 、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉 ，短期金融市場紛擾不止 ，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度 。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變 ，逢股市拉回時可分批布局 ，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間 ，殖利率偏高環境 使中短天期債券提供
吸引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後
利差具吸引力者，包含金融、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、通訊及公用事業等

匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。
美元高檔有撐
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期難解，油價維持高位但震盪加劇。
 短期美國雙率走高 ，使金價短期承壓 ，惟美歐日財政赤字壓力與去美元化 ，使全球央行與長期投資
人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法

### Page 18

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

STOXX 600 EARNINGS OUTLOOK  
EUROPEAN AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS

 Third quarter earnings are expected to increase 25.0% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, earnings 
are expected to increase 9.5%.

 Third quarter revenue is expected to increase 11.9% from Q3 2026. Excluding the Energy sector, revenues are 
expected to increase 6.9%.

 212 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported earnings to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 58.0% reported results 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 257 companies in the STOXX 600 have reported revenue to date for Q3 2026. Of these, 64.2% reported revenue 
exceeding analyst estimates.

 During the week of November 9, 40 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

Through November 5, 212 companies in the STOXX 600 Index reported earnings for Q3 2026. Of these, 58% reported 
earnings above analyst estimates. In a typical quarter (sin ce 2012), 54% of companies beat estimates and 39% miss 
estimates.

In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 4.2% above estimates, which is below the 5.9% long-term 
(since 2012) average surprise factor.

compared to Q3 2025.

2026, compared to earnings of €14.1B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (-69.6%) of any sector. It is expected to earn €0.9B in Q3 
2026, compared to earnings of €3.1B in Q3 2025.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1 -3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressu res, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect dir ect references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell -off and a Eurozone -
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisat ion of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026 –FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient expo rt 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy , slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following  
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full -year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12 –13% yoy , close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double -digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy  in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI -related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak s pot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

## Document 443: US SECTORS CALL_ Information Technology, Industrials & Utilities

Source PDF: all report pdf/US SECTORS CALL_ Information Technology, Industrials & Utilities.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-08
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US SECTORS CALL: Information Technology, Industrials & Utilities
Date:    21 minutes ago
US SECTORS CALL: Information Technology, Industrials & Utilities

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US SECTORS CALL: Information
Technology, Industrials &
Utilities
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 8 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Element5 Digital / Unsplash

### Page 2

Last Thursday’s selloff in the stock market was an AI selloff. Investors
dumped companies that build, wire, and power data centers, following the
Democrats' sweep of Congress amid voter hostility toward new facilities.
Stocks recovered some ground on Friday. October payrolls rose by
165,000, beating expectations of 65,000. Still, the S&P 500 ended the
week down 2.7%, and Information Technology (MW) fell 6.9% (chart).
Energy (OW) was the worst-performing sector this week, down 11.9%, for

a different reason. Oil prices tumbled on hopes of a US-Iran peace deal
following the midterm elections. We are retaining our overweight
recommendation on Energy as a hedge against re-escalation. Consumer
Discretionary (UW) and Communication Services (MW) led the week, up
2.7% and 2.3%.
The market is pricing the AI backlash as a roadblock. We see a speed
bump. Investors have already cut the multiples of the build-out’s suppliers.
Analysts have yet to cut their earnings estimates. That is the next test. We

are not changing any of our sector weights. Here’s more on Information
Technology, Industrials, and Utilities:
(1) Information Technology. The selloff hit the build-out’s hardware
suppliers the hardest. Semiconductor Equipment fell 14.5% this week,
followed by Semiconductors (-10.9%) and Communication Equipment
(-9.4%) (chart).

### Page 3

Semiconductor Equipment is the clearest case of a speed bump priced as
a roadblock. The industry’s stock price index is 27% below its 200-day
moving average (chart). Yet its forward earnings and forward profit margin
are at record highs. Its forward P/E has dropped to 21.2 from about 52.3
in June.
Analysts keep raising their estimates for Semiconductor Equipment
stocks. Collectively, they expect the industry’s earnings to grow 35.4% this
year and 51.5% next year (chart). Their consensus earnings estimate for

2027 has risen steadily all year. Semiconductor Equipment is one of only
two IT industries for which analysts expect faster growth next year than
this year.

### Page 4

Valuations are far from tech-bubble extremes. IT and Communication
Services together account for 47.6% of the S&P 500’s market
capitalization and a nearly identical 47.1% of its forward earnings (chart).
In 2000, when valuations were at tech-bubble extremes, their market-cap
share far exceeded their earnings share.
Tech's forward P/E has fallen to 17.3, and Semiconductors' forward P/E is
down to 12.0. That already discounts some earnings disappointment. A

modest cut in estimates is probably priced in. A deep one is not. That’s
why we remain at a market weight rating.
(2) Industrials. The AI-exposed industries led Industrials lower this week.
Electrical Components & Equipment fell 9.1%, and Construction

### Page 5

Machinery & Heavy Trucks fell 7.8% (chart). Passenger Airlines, Industrial
Conglomerates, and Industrial Machinery all rose.
Electrical Components shows the same disconnect as Semiconductor
Equipment. Its stock price index is 13.2% below its 200-day moving
average. Yet its forward revenues and forward earnings are at record
highs, and its forward profit margin rose to a record 18.2% (chart).
Analysts expect the industry’s earnings to grow 20.5% this year and

19.1% next year. Its forward P/E has fallen to 23.0 from over 30.
The Construction Machinery & Heavy Trucks index is trading at 26%
below its 200-day moving average, even as its forward earnings have
risen to a record high. Elsewhere, Rail Transportation is up 24.4% ytd.
Analysts have trimmed the sector’s 2027 earnings growth estimate to

### Page 6

15.8%, from 19.0% in July. That is still strong growth, and it supports our
overweight rating.
(3) Utilities. The gap between this sector’s best and worst performing
industries is enormous (chart). The Independent Power Producers &
Energy Traders industry stock price index is down 18.2% ytd, including a
7.8% drop this week. Water Utilities is up 15.2% ytd, while the Utilities
sector as a whole is up just 1.0% ytd.
IPPs sell power to data centers, and investors are pricing out that

premium before analysts do. The industry’s forward earnings is up 8.4%
ytd, while its stock price index is down 18.2% (chart). Its forward P/E has
fallen to 10.0, and its forward profit margin is at a record high.

### Page 7

Analysts are starting to follow investors’ lead. They cut their 2026 revenue
growth estimate for IPPs to 15.7% from 21.6% over the summer (chart).
But they still expect IPP earnings to grow 12.0% next year. The low
valuation suggests that investors expect more estimate cuts.
We downgraded Utilities to market weight on October 2 due to rising bond
yields. The data center premium coming out of the IPPs gives us a second
reason. We remain at a market weight rating.

clause.

## Document 451: What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity continues to turn the screw_ (2026-10-22).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
WHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM
THE MEASURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO
TURN THE SCREW?
Marianne Mueller and Pascal Devaux
issue 19
21 October 2026
The 2022 energy crisis clearly highlighted the issue of electricity prices in Europe — and, more broadly, the issue of energy prices. As
a result, European manufacturers’ competitiveness has been severely undermined when compared with the United States and China.

By reigniting tensions over electricity prices, the conflict that began in the Middle East in late February 2026 further underscores the
continent’s vulnerability to fossil fuel imports, despite the expansion of renewable energy. Faced with an environment of potential sharp
and sudden price rises, European governments do not have the same room for manoeuvre.
While the additional costs borne by European manufacturers are mainly linked to the share of gas in national electricity mixes, we will

first illustrate that, in Germany, grid operation and maintenance costs, as well as taxation, are also contributing significantly to these
costs.
We will then explore the solutions proposed by the German government under the new European measures regulating state aid to
electricity-intensive industries.
Finally, our estimates will show that, while these measures make Germany more competitive against some European countries, they
do not place it on an equal competitive footing with the United States or China. Nevertheless, Germany has made significant progress

in decarbonising and making its electricity mix more flexible from a European perspective. This should help to ensure that electricity costs
are reduced in the medium term.

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 19 / 21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldWHAT LESSONS CAN WE LEARN TO KEEP EUROPEAN INDUSTRY COMPETITIVE FROM THE MEA- SURES TAKEN BY GERMANY, AS THE COST OF ELECTRICITY CONTINUES TO TURN THE SCREW?
GAS: A KEY FACTOR IN THE ADDITIONAL ELECTRICITY COSTS
FACED BY EUROPEAN INDUSTRY
European manufacturers are still paying significantly higher elec -
tricity costs than their American and Chinese competitors. In 2019,

there was a 30% difference between the prices charged to European
electricity-intensive industries (corresponding to the lower end of the
industrial price range) and the average US industrial price. In 2025,
it stood at nearly 100% according to the IEA (2026) 1, after peaking at
an average of 148% between 2022 and 2023. There is currently a 50%
difference with their Chinese competitors2.
The disruption to Russian gas supplies since 2022 is behind the rising

gas prices, particularly as the EU has had to turn to liquefied natural
gas3 (LNG), which is more expensive than gas transported via pipe -
line and, above all, exposes Europe to global gas market conditions.
The growth of LNG has, in fact, intensified competition between the
major consumption regions, primarily Europe and Asia. European and
Asian prices now tend to move more in tandem (Chart 1). However, in
China, the prevalence of coal — a widely available local resource —

and a more regulated electricity price help to keep industrial prices
lower. At the same time, the United States enjoys plentiful domestic
gas, which is produced at a low cost, driven by the sharp rise in shale
oil and gas production over the past decade. By contrast, since 2022,
Europe has been more exposed to the cost of imported gas (90% of its
total consumption, according to Eurostat), while the United States and
China have enjoyed plentiful domestic resources.

Fluctuations and the level of gas prices are passed on to Eu -
ropean electricity prices via the ‘merit order’ marginal pricing
mechanism, with the wholesale price determined by the margi -
nal cost of the last (and most expensive) electricity source called
upon to meet demand.
As a result, gas plays a decisive role, with gas-fired power sta -
tions setting the price in around 40% of cases in Europe4, with si-
gnificant variations depending on national electricity mixes. It is

close to this average in Germany, below it in Spain (26%) and
France (17%), and above it in Italy (73%) 5 in 2025 6 7. Therefore,
electricity prices are still sensitive to fluctuations in gas prices,
even when electricity is not generated from gas (Chart 2). This
pass-through is particularly pronounced when demand rises
and requires the use of more expensive sources, or when supply
constraints arise (for example, during extreme heatwaves that
reduce wind-power generation or disrupt the cooling of nuclear

power plants).
1 Prices – Electricity 2026 – Analysis – IEA
2 The difference may be even larger for other manufacturers who do not receive the same support as electricity-intensive sectors.
3 Imports of Russian gas (via pipeline and LNG) accounted for around 45% of total European imports in 2021, compared with 14% in Q2 2026 (Bruegel, natural gas imports).
4 ACER, 2026, Key developments in EU electricity and gas markets, 2026 Monitoring report.
5 In Italy, in response to sharply rising gas prices in 2026, the government is currently negotiating a potential option of capping the prices of gas supplied to power stations in

order to limit the rise in the wholesale price of electricity. However, this can only be a temporary general mechanism, with the potentially major cost being borne by public funds
and/or households.
6 Eurelectric, 2026, Power Barometer.
7 EMBER, 2026, Latest energy shock reminds Europe of its risky reliance on gas.
8 Energy shock: 2026 vs. 2022 dashboard – 2nd October 2026 – Economic Studies – BNP Paribas
However, for the time being, the 2026 energy crisis is having a far

smaller effect on electricity prices than in 2022. On the one hand, the
2026 peak reached by the European TTF benchmark is still well below
that recorded in 20228.
In addition, there has been significant progress in decarbonising Eu -
ropean electricity mixes since 2022, which has helped to mitigate, on
average, the pass-through of gas prices to electricity prices.
